3) Flush Wurtec phone mounted behind the car station  
4) 1 digital position indicator w 2’’ red characters per elevator if present  
5) 1 ¼’’ square, #4 stainless steel braille in black   
6) Push buttons = alarm, door open, door close, phone push to call button and hall calls  
7) Key switches = Emergency stop switch, light switch, fan (2 speed) switch, independent 
service, emergency light test switch, hoistway access switch & universal FEOK1 phase II 
fire service key switch  
8) Engravings = “Phase II fire service instructions, capacity # & elevator number.  
9) Misc. = Buzzer for fire service & nudging, floor passing signal (in digital PI), fireman’s 
service cabinet, emergency LED lighting & voice annunciator -  
 
 
 
Hall stations (buttons, etc. outside in hallway):  
1) Two terminal hall stations UP button on level “1” WITH FEOK1 fire service key switch 
2) One intermediate hall station (front & rear) with UP and DOWN button on level 2 
3) Stainless steel round projecting buttons w outer white illumination  
4) Each button equipped with 1 ¼’’ square, #4 brushed stainless-steel braille on black  
5) Hoistway access key switches on levels 1 & 3  
 
Hall lanterns/Position indicators (arrows/numbers outside in hallway located above door):

2) One intermediate hall lantern on floor 2 if existing 
3) One terminal-down hall lantern on floor 3 if existing 
4) All hall lanterns will be faced with #4 SS plates and have a 2 ½’’ projecting arrow w LED 
white lighting

1) Digital PI controller / Encoder / Driver- MCCA (Component used to communicate w 
controller)  
2) Jamb braille tags. 4’’ x 4’’, adhesive backed, #4 stainless steel on black- Currently, no jamb 
braille exists. This would be required per code. This is the numbers that would be stuck to

TERMS AND CONDITIONS 
This proposal is subject to the following terms and conditions, all of which are hereby agreed to: 
KONE shall submit invoices for the value of material delivered and/or labor performed, less the down payment paid at the time of proposal acceptance. A final 
invoice shall be issued by KONE upon completion of the work and shall include all balances due. Purchaser agrees to pay the amount of any tax imposed by 
any existing law, or by any law enacted after the date of this Agreement, based upon the transfer, use, ownership or possession of the equipment involved in 
the services rendered herein. KONE reserves the right to discontinue our work at anytime until we have assurance, satisfactory to us, that payments will be 
made as agreed. Final payment shall become due and payable upon completion of the work described in this Agreement. Failure to pay any sum due to KONE 
within thirty (30) days of the invoice will be a material breach. A delinquent payment charge calculated at the rate of 1½ % per month, or if such rate is usurious 
then at the maximum rate under applicable law, shall be applied to the delinquent payments. In the event of default on the payment provisions herein, Purchaser 
agrees to pay, in addition to any defaulted amount, all attorney fees, collection cost or court costs in connection therewith. The machinery, implements and 
apparatus furnished hereunder remain KONE’s personal property and KONE retains title thereto until final payment is made, with right to retake possession of 
the same at the cost of the Purchaser if default is made in any of the payments, irrespective of the manner of attachment to the realty, the acceptance of notes, 
or the sale, mortgage or lease of the premises. 
The states requiring notice prior to filing a lien, this notice requirement is hereby complied with. 
KONE shall not be liable for damage or delay caused directly or indirectly by accidents, embargoes, strikes, lockouts, work interruption or other labor dispute, 
fire, theft, floods, or any cause beyond KONE’s control.  Regardless of the type of delay, KONE shall not be liable for any indirect, consequential, or special 
damages including but not limited to fines, penalties, loss of profits, goodwill, business or loss of use of equipment or property. 
Purchaser agrees to provide safe access to the equipment and machine room areas. Should conditions develop beyond KONE’s control, making the building or 
premises in which KONE’s personnel are working unsafe, KONE reserves the right to discontinue work until such unsafe conditions are corrected.  Should 
damage occur to KONE’s material or work on the premises, by fire, theft, vandalism, or other causes beyond KONE’s reasonable control, Purchaser shall 
compensate KONE for such damage.   
Any asbestos removal necessitated by work described in this Proposal will be the Purchaser’s responsibility. Purchaser shall provide documentation that the 
asbestos has been abated from the KONE work area and air clearance reports shall be made available upon request. Purchaser is responsible for all costs of 
oil disposal should it be determined that oil from Purchaser’s equipment is contaminated. 
KONE undertakes to perform this work in conformity with the usual applied codes and standards, however, no guarantee can be made that all code violations or 
defects have been found.  This work is not intended as a guarantee against failure or malfunction of equipment at any future time. 
It is agreed and understood that KONE is not responsible for damages, either to the vertical transportation equipment or to the building, or for any personal injury 
or death, arising from or resulting from any code required safety tests performed on this equipment. 
Nothing in this agreement shall be construed to mean that KONE assumes any liability of any nature whatsoever arising out of, relating to or in any way connected 
with the use or operation of the equipment described above.  Purchaser shall be solely responsible for the use, repair and maintenance of the equipment and 
for taking such steps including but not limited to providing attendant personnel, warning signs and other controls necessary to ensure the safety of the user or 
safe operation of the equipment. 
Neither KONE nor its affiliates, subsidiaries or divisions shall be responsible or liable for any damages, claims, suits, expenses and payments on account of or 
resulting from any injury, death or damage to property arising or resulting from the misuse, abuse or neglect of the equipment herein named or any other device 
covered by this contract. 
Purchaser shall at all times and at Purchaser’s own cost, maintain liability coverage covering bodily injury and property damage with the limits of liability 
Purchasers customarily carry arising out of the services provided under this Authorization and/or the ownership, maintenance, use or operation of the equipment 
described herein.  
It is agreed and understood that Purchaser is solely responsible for ongoing maintenance and care of the equipment described above.  IT IS EXPRESSLY 
UNDERSTOOD, IN CONSIDERATION OF OUR PERFORMANCE OF THIS WORK THAT PURCHASER ASSUMES ALL LIABILITY FOR THE USE, 
MAINTENANCE OR OPERATION OF THE EQUIPMENT DESCRIBED ABOVE AND FOR ANY INJURY, INCLUDING DEATH, TO ANY PERSON OR 
PERSONS AND FOR DAMAGE TO PROPERTY OR LOSS OF USE THEREOF, ON ACCOUNT OF OR RESULTING FROM THE PERFORMANCE OF THE 
WORK TO BE DONE. NEITHER PURCHASER OR KONE INDEMNIFIES THE OTHER AND BOTH SHALL BE RESPONSIBLE FOR THEIR OWN ACTIONS 
AS LONG AS THOSE ACTIONS ARE IN ACCORDANCE WITH STANDARD OPERATING PROCEDURES. Purchaser hereby waives any and all rights of 
recovery, arising as a matter of law or otherwise, which Purchaser might now or hereafter have against KONE Inc. 
 
KONE warrants the materials and workmanship of the equipment for ONE (1) year after completion. Purchaser’s remedy is limited to repair or replacement of a 
defective part, in KONE’s sole discretion. The warranty is limited to the replacement or repair of the part itself, and labor. In no event shall KONE be 
responsible for damage due to normal wear and tear, vandalism, abuse, misuse, neglect, work or repairs or modifications by others, or any other cause 
beyond the control of KONE. KONE disclaims any other warranty of any kind, either expressed or implied, including without limitation the implied warranties of 
merchantability or fitness for a particular purpose, or noninfringement. 
Unless otherwise agreed, it is understood that the work shall be performed during regular working hours of regular working days of the elevator trade. If 
overtime work is mutually agreed upon and performed, the additional price, at KONE’s usual rates for such work, shall be added to the contract price herein 
named. 
It is expressly understood and agreed all prior agreements written or verbal regarding the subject matter herein are void and the acceptance of this Agreement 
shall constitute the contract for the material and work specified in this Agreement. Any changes to this Agreement must be made in writing and signed by both 
parties. 
The terms and conditions set forth herein shall constitute the complete agreement for any work performed, AND shall prevail over and supersede any terms 
and conditions contained in any documents provided by the Purchaser.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2025-10-03_TotalExtraction_31164941.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
September 18, 2025 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #S-2-26 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and 
Richardson Construction, Inc., for the Depot Renovation Project  
______________________________________________ 
Bid #S-2-26 was solicited for the Depot Renovation Project. 
 
The Project involves the renovation of a portion of the Second and Third Levels and 
Exterior Improvements at the Historic UP Depot building located at 121 West 15th Street, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming. The Project involves furnishing all labor, materials, services, and equipment 
required for the project, including, but not limited to selective demolition, site work, work on historic 
wood windows, finishes, electrical, and mechanical work. 
The Community Recreation and Events Department has elected to remove the Historic Masonry 
Repairs from the current Project Scope. As a result, the Contract value for Richardson Construction, 
the lowest responsive bidder, has been revised to $1,874,421.00, reflecting a cost reduction of 
$419,579.00. Following consultation with the project Architect, it has been confirmed that deferring 
the Historic Masonry Repairs will not result in significant deterioration concerns and can be addressed 
at a future date. 
Four (4) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.  The Bids were received 
from: 
1. Recco, Inc. dba Five-R Co. 
2. Reiman Corp. 
3. Richardson Construction, Inc 
4. Sampson Construction 
 
The bids were reviewed by Jason Sanchez (CRE), Sam Payne (CRE), and The Design Studio, Inc 
(TDSi), and their recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid from Richardson 
Construction, Inc. 
 
The total Bid Award is $1,874,421.00.   
 
Funding:

CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
AGREEMENT 
 
Bid No. S-3-26 
 
Contract No. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, 2025, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and CHEENEY 
LANDSCAPING, INC., hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the “High Plains Arboretum - Irrigation Project” project all in 
strict accordance with the Contract Documents, including all addenda thereto, numbered and 
dated: Addendum One (1), dated September 17, 2025. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract, or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City. The work shall be completed by June 30, 2026. If the work has not been completed 
within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City for excusable 
delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated damages, as 
stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the work is 
completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, for the base bid in the 
amount of one hundred eighty-eight thousand, two hundred twenty-one dollars 
($188,221.00), for the booster pump (if required) in an amount of forty-eight thousand, four 
hundred twenty dollars ($48,420.00), for a total not to exceed two hundred thirty-six 
thousand, six hundred forty-one dollars ($236,641.00).  In the event there are changes in the 
estimated quantities shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices multiplied by the actual quantities 
shall govern, and the total contract amount will be adjusted accordingly. The City agrees to pay 
the above amount for contractual services in the following manner, upon receipt of appropriate 
documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for the percentage of 
estimated work completed. Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the 
payable due date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office. The pay request 
9:32 am, Oct 06, 2025

Page 2 of 2 
shall be submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or 
the AIA Documents G702 and G703. The engineer will review the estimate for approval 
prior to payment. 
 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116. Upon completion of the work 
under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of Completion; 
the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all Sub-Contractors, 
Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of Payment; and a 
current Workers Compensation Certificate. Final payment will not be made until the above 
documents have been received by the City, all items on the Punch List have been 
completed, and the advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following: 
 
1. This Agreement;  
2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation for Bids; 
4. Instructions to Bidders; 
5. Signed Bid Proposal; 
 
6. General Conditions and Insurance; 
7. Supplemental Conditions; 
8. Part IV Forms & Notices; 
9. Specifications and Special 
Provisions; and  
10. Drawings. 
 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said 
other documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms 
the Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, that the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has authorized 
the Mayor as Executive Officer of the City to enter into this Agreement, and that the parties hereto 
have caused this Agreement to be executed on the day and year in the first part herein written.

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE ANNEXING TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING, LAND NORTHEAST OF THE INTERSECTION OF 
FRONTIER MALL DRIVE AND RUE TERRE AVENUE AND WEST OF 
CONVERSE AVENUE." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the City of Cheyenne hereby finds as follows: 
 
(a) 
That Section 20 LLC ET AL, is the owner of the below-described real property and 
has petitioned the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the property 
described herein and to make it a part of the City: 
 
Tract 2, Winco Subdivision and land beginning at a point on the West right 
of way line of Converse Avenue, also being a point on the North line of said 
Southeast Quarter, from which the East Quarter Corner of said Section 20 
bears; S89°57'17"E, a distance of 478.18 feet; thence along a non-tangential 
curve, said curve turning to the left through an angle of 12° 16' 20", having 
a radius of 875.59 feet, and whose long chord bears S11° 42' 22"E, a 
distance of 187.18 feet, along said West right of way line, to a point of 
intersection with a non-tangential line, said intersection being the Northwest 
intersection of said Converse Avenue and East Carlson Street; thence along 
the North right of way line of East Carlson Street for the next three courses, 
S66° 20' 36"W, a distance of 47.47 feet to the beginning of a non-tangential 
curve, said curve turning to the right through an angle of 60° 07' 14", having 
a radius of 262.00 feet, and whose long chord bears N83° 31' 50"W for a 
distance of 262.48 feet; thence N53° 28' 14"W , a distance of 290.91 feet to 
the North line of said Southeast Quarter; thence, S89° 57' 17"E, a distance 
of 500.06 feet  to the point of beginning. Containing 1.83 acres more or less. 
 
(b) 
That an accurate annexation map of said land is attached to this ordinance as 
Exhibit A, said map prepared by Jeffrey B. Jones, a Professional Land Surveyor, 
licensed in the State of Wyoming, PLS Number 5910; 
 
(c) 
That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare 
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; 
 
(d) 
That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; 
 
(e) 
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and other 
services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, be 
furnished; 
10:20 am, Aug 04, 2025

 
(f) 
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned CB – 
Community Business by the Laramie County Commissioners; and 
 
(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and its 
governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the entire annexed 
area. 
 
Section 2.  That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
 
Section 3.  That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance, together with the map identified as Exhibit A, with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio 
Register of Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office 
to add said area to the official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Section 4.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared illegal 
or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining 
phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 5.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

CITY OF CHEYENNE GOVERNING BODY
AUGUST 11, 2025
STAFF REPORT
FILE:
PUDC-25-80: Tract 2 Winco Subdivision and land west of Converse Avenue -
Annexation
REQUEST:
An annexation of 18.62 acres of land.
LOCATION:
Northeast of the intersection of Frontier Mall Dr. and Rue Terre Avenue and 
west of Converse Avenue.
APPLICANT:
Shane Hanson, Stiel Surveying
AGENT:
Shane Hanson, Stiel Surveying
OWNERS:
Section 20 LLC
PURPOSE:
Commercial Development
PREPARED BY:
Connor White, Planner II
Planning and Development Department
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

PUDC-25-80 
 
 
August 11, 2025 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting annexing 18.62 acres of land to allow for commercial development. The land is 
currently vacant. The annexed area will be assigned a City zone based on the underlying County zone. No 
other applications have been submitted for this property at this time.  
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. The petition to annex has been accompanied by an assigned 
City zone of CB Community Business.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the future 
occupants. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to City Fire and Police and 
sanitation services. By being within City Limits the property will be able to connect to City 
services.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)]

PUDC-25-80 
 
 
August 11, 2025 
These properties are currently vacant but are within a major commercial area of the City. They 
are surrounded by urban development making these properties a natural, geographic, 
economic, and social part of the city. The properties are adjacent to the City to the south, east, 
and west. The contiguity to the City and the proximity to surrounding urban-style development 
makes the parcel a natural geographic part of the City. The annexation of these properties will 
benefit those who live within the City and will be a part of the social and economic fabric that is 
Cheyenne. These parcels are part of a larger County Pocket.    
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. Utilities are within the area.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
Tract 2, Winco Subdivision is roughly 69% contiguous. The parcel adjacent to Converse Avenue 
and E Carlson is roughly 62% contiguous.    
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on July 10, 2025. Property 
owners within 300-feet of the area to be annexed were also mailed notice on July 10, 2025. The 
public hearing for this annexation is scheduled for August 11, 2025. Twenty (20) business days 
(on the City’s calendar) prior to the announced public hearing is July 11, 2025. The earliest 
confirmation of delivery was dated July 12, 2025. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   

PUDC-25-80 
 
 
August 11, 2025 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with Commercial uses. These areas are expected to develop with full access to 
urban services – such areas are best served by being within City limits. The area is within the Urban 
Service Boundary. 
 
PUBLIC NOTICE AND PUBLIC COMMENTS RECEIVED: 
Although projects have public notice and the opportunity for public comments, projects are required to 
be approved or denied based on the review criteria (listed above). Public comments relevant to the 
review criteria may assist the review and discussion on the application. 
  
The public was given notice of the project in the following ways: 
- 
Legal Notice in the Wyoming Tribune Eagle published on July 12, 2025 and August 2, 2025. 
- 
Mailed Notice was sent out by Staff on July 10, 2025 to all property owners within the 
annexation and within 300 feet of the area to be annexed.  
- 
Posted Notice Evidence of posted notice was provided that signs were posted on May 20, 2025.  
- 
Internet Posting on connect.cheyennecity.org on July 14, 2025. 
 
As of the publication of the Staff Report (July 28, 2025), Staff has received one inquiry from the public on 
this project. The inquiry was just about what may be planned and there was no objection. The project 
page on the website has received 15 views and no comments. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
No major comments were received.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
services typically available to urban areas are available to be extended to this property.  
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Submitted Application 
4. Applicant-submitted annexation map

MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Connor White 
Subject: PUDC-25-80 | Winco Subdivision – Assigned Zoning  
Date: 
August 11, 2025 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is an Assigned Zoning.  This is approved by Ordinance.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral  
August 11, 2025 
• 
Public Services Committee  
August 18, 2025 
• 
Second reading  
 
August 25, 2025 
• 
Public Services Committee  
September 2, 2025 
• 
Third and final reading   
September 8, 2025 
 
Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their August 4, 2025 meeting and unanimously 
recommended the Governing Body approve this application.  
 
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached and has not been changed. 
 
Public Comment 
As of the creation of this memo (August 6, 2025), Staff has received no inquires or comments 
from the public on this project. The project page on the website has received 24 views and no 
comments. 
 
Related Items: 
 
• 
PUDC-25-80: Annexation 
 
Attachments: 
1. Ordinance 
2. Planning Commission Staff Report

2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY 
OF 
CHEYENNE, 
ESTABLISHING 
THE 
ZONING 
CLASSIFICATION OF CB – COMMUNITY BUSINESS FOR LAND 
ANNEXED TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE LOCATED NORTHEAST OF 
THE INTERSECTION OF FRONTIER MALL DRIVE AND RUE TERRE 
AVENUE AND WEST OF CONVERSE AVENUE.” 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  Section 20 LLC ET AL, is the owner of the below-described real property and 
has petitioned the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne to annex the property described herein 
and to make it a part of the City: 
 
Tract 2, Winco Subdivision and land beginning at a point on the West right of way 
line of Converse Avenue, also being a point on the North line of said Southeast 
Quarter, from which the East Quarter Corner of said Section 20 bears; S89°57'17"E, 
a distance of 478.18 feet; thence along a non-tangential curve, said curve turning to 
the left through an angle of 12° 16' 20", having a radius of 875.59 feet, and whose 
long chord bears S11° 42' 22"E, a distance of 187.18 feet, along said West right of 
way line, to a point of intersection with a non-tangential line, said intersection being 
the Northwest intersection of said Converse Avenue and East Carlson Street; thence 
along the North right of way line of East Carlson Street for the next three courses, 
S66° 20' 36"W, a distance of 47.47 feet to the beginning of a non-tangential curve, 
said curve turning to the right through an angle of 60° 07' 14", having a radius of 
262.00 feet, and whose long chord bears N83° 31' 50"W for a distance of 262.48 
feet; thence N53° 28' 14"W , a distance of 290.91 feet to the North line of said 
Southeast Quarter; thence, S89 57' 17"E, a distance of 500.06 feet to the point of 
beginning. Containing 1.83 acres more or less. 
 
 
Section 2.  That Section 2.2.8 of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (UDC) provides 
standards and procedures for zoning districts established on land or property currently without a 
City zone assignment, including land being annexed to the city.  
 
 
Section 3.  That on August 4, 2025, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
public hearings, reviewed the proposed zoning assignment that is the subject of this ordinance, and 
recommended approval of said zoning assignment.  
 
 
Section 4.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning 
assignment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.8.d of 
the UDC. 
 
10:30 am, Aug 04, 2025

 
Section 5.  That in accordance with Section 2.2.8, Assigned Zoning, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the zoning 
classifications of the real property as described in Section 1 is hereby established as CB – 
Community Business. 
 
 
Section 6.  That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 5 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 8 below. 
 
 
Section 7.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 8.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication, and upon approval and publication of the affiliated annexation.

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
AUGUST 4, 2025 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
 
PUDC-25-80: Tract 2 Winco Subdivision and land west of Converse 
Avenue – Assigned Zoning 
LOCATION: 
   Northeast of the intersection of Frontier Mall Dr. and Rue Terre Avenue 
and west of Converse Avenue.  
REQUEST: 
  Assigning the CB Community Business zone to a property that has 
petitioned for annexation. 
ANNEXATION 
APPLICANT: 
   Shane Hanson, Stiel Surveying 
OWNER: 
Section 20 LLC 
CURRENT ZONING: 
County CB  
PROPOSED 
ZONING: 
CB Community Business 
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Mixed-use Commercial 
PURPOSE: 
To establish City zoning district concurrent with annexation. 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-25-80 
 
 
August 4, 2025 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve the assigned zoning for northeast of the intersection 
of Frontier Mall Dr. and Rue Terre Avenue and west of Converse Avenue to CB Community Business as 
shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.8 Assigned Zoning 
 
BACKGROUND: 
The applicant has petitioned for roughly 18.62 acres of land to be annexed into city limits. The land proposed 
to be annexed is Tract 2, Winco Subdivision and a 1.83 acre parcel west of Converse Avenue and north of E 
Carlson Street. Annexation of a parcel requires a city zone to be established. Per UDC 2.2.8 a zone will be 
assigned based on the underlying county zone and per the policy document adopted by the city. Should the 
applicant wish to have a different zone at the time of annexation, they will be required to submit a 
subsequent zoning map amendment, proposing a different zone. The applicant is not requesting a different 
City zone at this time. Prior to this annexation the applicant went through a zone change in the County where 
the County Commissioners approved the rezoning of these two parcels to CB Community Business. 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The assigned zone is the most analogous City zone to the current zone when considering 
allowed uses. Zoning for standard County zones shall be assigned based on the policy 
document, while zones not listed on the policy document and terminated City zones shall be 
assigned a City zone on a case-by-case basis. (UDC 2.2.8.d.1) 
 
The underlying county zone is CB Community Business. The most analogous city zone is CB 
Community Business according to the policy document. The Assigned Zoning policy document is 
attached to the staff report as attachment number two.  
 
Establishment of the City zone matches the policy document; therefore, the item complies with 
review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, there were no comments.  
 
PUBLIC NOTICE AND PUBLIC COMMENTS RECEIVED: 
Although projects have public notice and the opportunity for public comments, projects are required to 
be approved or denied based on the review criteria (listed above). Public comments relevant to the 
review criteria may assist the review and discussion on the application. 
  
The public was given notice of the project in the following ways: 
- 
Legal Notice in the Wyoming Tribune Eagle published on July 19, 2025. 

PUDC-25-80 
 
 
August 4, 2025 
- 
Mailed Notice was sent out by Staff on July 10, 2025 to all property owners within the 
annexation and within 300 feet of the area to be annexed.  
- 
Posted Notice Evidence of posted notice was provided that signs were posted on May 20, 2025.  
- 
Internet Posting on connect.cheyennecity.org on July 14, 2025. 
 
As of the publication of the Staff Report (July 28, 2025), Staff has received one inquiry from the public on 
this project. The inquiry was just about what may be planned and there was no objection. The project 
page on the website has received 15 views and no comments. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.8.(d). 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the Assigned Zoning based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the Assigned Zoning until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or recommends conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for their recommendation. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the assigned zoning for northeast of the intersection of Frontier Mall Dr. 
and Rue Terre Avenue and west of Converse Avenue to CB Community Business as shown in the 
attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
ATTACHMENTS:

2. Assigned Zoning Policy Document 
3. Use Differences

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FOR LAND LOCATED SOUTHWEST OF THE INTERSECTION OF 
HOLLAND COURT AND RIDGE ROAD FROM AG – AGRICULTURAL 
TO NR-2 – NEIGHBORHOOD RESIDENTIAL – MEDIUM DENSITY." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That the owner of real property located southwest of the intersection of Holland 
Court and Ridge Road has submitted an application for a zoning map amendment for said property 
in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development 
Code ("UDC"). 
 
 
Section 2.  That on August 4, 2025, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zoning map amendment that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zoning map amendment. 
 
 
Section 3.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning map 
amendment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of 
the UDC. 
 
 
Section 4.  That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the zoning 
classification of the following described real property is hereby amended and reclassified to NR-2 
Neighborhood Residential – Medium Density: 
 
DELL RANGE ADDITION: SOUTH 1/2 TRACT 25 LESS THE SOUTH 144-1/2' 
PLUS VACATED BLOCK 2 (BK 1149 PG 304), ALLEY BETWEEN LOTS 1-8 
AND 9-16, GROVE AVE AND SMITH CT, HOLLAND-SMITH SUBDIVISION 
(BK 1071 PG 275) DESC AS (BK 1071 PG 278): BEG AT A PNT LOCATED ON 
THE WEST LINE OF SEC 22 WITH SAID STARTING PNT BEING 379' 
SOUTH OF THE SEC COR MARKING THE NW COR OF SAID SEC 22; TH 
PROCEEDING SOUTHERLY FROM SAID STARTING PNT ALONG THE 
WEST LINE OF SAID SEC 22, 733.29'; TH PROCEEDING EASTERLY 
ALONG THE LINE SEPARATING THE SOUTH 144-1/2' OF THE ORIGINAL 
TRACT 25, FROM THE REMAINDER OF SAID TRACT 25, 620', ARRIVING 
AT A PNT ON THE EAST LINE OF SAID TRACT 25; TH PROCEEDING 
NORTHERLY ALONG THIS EAST LINE OF SAID TRACT 25, PARALLEL 
TO THE WEST LINE OF SAID SEC 22, 734'; AND FINALLY PROCEEDING 
WESTERLY ON THE LINE NOW FORMED BY THE NORTH EDGE OF 
BLOCK 2 OF HOLLAND-SMITH SUBDIVISION OF THE NORTH 1/2 OF 
10:29 am, Aug 04, 2025

SAID TRACT 25, 620' TO THE STARTING PNT, WHICH IS 379' SOUTH OF 
SAID SEC COR AND WHICH PNT LIES ON THE WEST LINE OF SAID SEC 
22. 
 
 
Section 5.  That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 7 below. 
 
 
Section 6.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 7.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication.

STAFF REPORT

change)  
REQUEST: 
A request to rezone approximately 10.94 acres of land from  
AG Agricultural to NR-2 Neighborhood Residential – Medium Density 
LOCATION: 
Southwest of the intersection of Holland Ct. and Ridge Road. 
APPLICANT: 
Bedrock Development LLC 
AGENT: 
AVI PC

Bedrock Development LLC

AG Agricultural 
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Urban Residential 
PURPOSE: 
To allow for Residential Development.   
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-25-151 
 
 
August 4, 2025 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of land located southwest of the intersection of Holland Ct. and Ridge 
Road to NR-2 Neighborhood Residential – Medium Density as shown in the attached zone change map, 
noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
• 
UDC Article 5 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from AG Agricultural to NR-2 Neighborhood 
Residential – Medium Density for roughly 10.94 acres of land. The land is currently vacant. The current AG 
zone was assigned by the City as a holding zone until the Developer determined a zone that would allow for 
development of the parcel.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, in an area with 
infrastructure that is currently being built.   
 
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Urban Residential and the surrounding area 
is Urban Residential and Urban Transition Residential. According to the comprehensive plan the 
Future Land Use Designation

Subject 
Site 
Urban Residential

Vacant

Urban Residential

PUDC-25-151 
 
 
August 4, 2025 
optimal City zones for the subject property’s future land use designation is any one of the 
residential zones, including NR-2. 
 
Based on the policies and future land use map, NR-2 zoning is appropriate at this location.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
Surrounding properties in the City are zoned MR and AG. There is also a property zoned NR-3 to 
the northeast at the intersection of Ridge Rd. and Storey Blvd. MR and NR-2 zones are medium-
density residential zones. The difference is that NR-2 zoning anticipates alley-loaded 
development, allows smaller lots, more urban styles of development, and has a higher design 
standard. NR-2 zoning is compatible with surrounding zoning.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The AG zone was assigned to this property as a holding zone and would only allow for 
agricultural type uses. NR-2 zoning is intended for smaller-block, rear-loaded, compact 
development patterns (like what is found in the Avenues). The NR-2 zone allows for a variety of 
residential type uses and allows Cottage Lots as a use by right, which is a development style that 
has become more common recently.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The subject property will need to be platted in the future and will need to meet the standards of 
Article 4. Due to the size of the parcel, staff believes the standards can be met.   
 
Infrastructure (streets and utilities) are in the area and new streets and utilities can be extended 
to this parcel. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The NR-2 zone is intended for a medium density and mix of dwelling types that promote a 
consistent neighborhood character, with a compact development pattern and highly connected 

PUDC-25-151 
 
 
August 4, 2025 
street network. Due to the size of the lot, staff believes the applicant will be able to plat the 
property in a way that meets the intent of the zone district proposed. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The NR-2 zone allows a variety of residential uses. The NR-2 also has design standards required 
per UDC 6.6.4 which will need to be met for any residential development that is proposed for 
this area.   
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
Proposal meets comprehensive plan principles. No other comments were received from 
reviewers.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
PUBLIC NOTICE AND PUBLIC COMMENTS RECEIVED: 
Although projects have public notice and the opportunity for public comments, projects are required to 
be approved or denied based on the review criteria (listed above). Public comments relevant to the 
review criteria may assist the review and discussion on the application. 
  
The public was given notice of the project in the following ways [only those applicable to the application 
are listed]: 
• 
Legal Notice in the Wyoming Tribune Eagle published on July 19, 2025 
• 
Mailed Notice to neighbors within 300’, sent on July 19, 2025 (Mailed by applicant or their 
agent) 
• 
Posted Notice on the property was posted by the applicant on July 25, 2025. 
• 
Internet Posting on connect.cheyennecity.org on July 14, 2025. 
 

PUDC-25-151 
 
 
August 4, 2025 
As of the publication of the Staff Report (July 28, 2025), Staff has received no inquires or comments from 
the public on this project. The project page on the website has received 15 views and no comments.  
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no major review comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The NR-2 zone 
is appropriate based on the future land use map and surrounding zoning. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from AG to NR-2.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2025-08-05_TotalExtraction_85132718.pdf
 
Page 1 of 5 
 
ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN 
ORDINANCE 
CREATING 
SECTION 
10.24.200, 
VEHICLE 
MUFFLERS, EXCESSIVE SMOKE AND UNREASONABLE NOISE, AND 
REPEALING SECTION 8.56.050, MOTOR VEHICLE MUFFLERS, OF 
THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, TO 
REVISE AND ENHANCE PROVISIONS GOVERNING VEHICLE 
MUFFLERS AND NOISE, TO ESTABLISH A GRADUATED PENALTY 
STRUCTURE 
FOR 
RECURRING 
OFFENSES, 
AND 
TO 
MAKE 
CONFORMING AMENDMENTS AS SPECIFIED." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That Section 10.24.200, Vehicle mufflers, excessive smoke and unreasonable 
noise, of Chapter 10.24, Rules of the Road Generally, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby created to read: 
 
10.24.200 – Vehicle mufflers, excessive smoke and unreasonable noise. 
 
A. Every motor vehicle shall at all times be equipped with a muffler or other effective noise 
suppressing system in good working order and in constant operation. No person shall use 
a muffler cut-out, bypass or similar device. 
 
B. The engine and power mechanism of every motor vehicle shall be so equipped and adjusted 
as to prevent the escape of excessive fumes or smoke. 
 
C. No person shall make, continue to make, or cause any unreasonable noise originating in or 
from any vehicle. In addition, no person shall knowingly allow any vehicle they own, 
operate, or control to produce unreasonable noise, or operate any vehicle, regardless of 
ownership, in a manner or with equipment that produces unreasonable noise. 
 
D. Any peace officer enforcing this section is empowered to make a prima facie determination 
as to whether a noise is unreasonable based on consideration of one (1) or more of the 
following factors: 
 
1. The time of day at which the noise occurs; 
 
2. The location where the vehicle is being operated; 
 
3. The presence of pedestrians, bystanders, or other vehicles in the vicinity; 
 
4. The operation of a motor vehicle in a manner that causes unreasonable noise, including 
but not limited to unnecessary or rapid acceleration or deceleration, abrupt steering or 
turning, revving the engine, or tire squealing; 
 
4:09 pm, Aug 08, 2025

 
Page 2 of 5 
 
5. The operation of a vehicle with equipment improperly installed, removed or otherwise 
altered so as to produce unreasonable noise. This includes, but is not limited to 
unlawful, defective or modified exhaust systems, vehicles lacking a functioning 
muffler or exhaust system, or any other modification to any part of the vehicle that 
increases noise output; 
 
6. The presence or use of noise amplification equipment, including stereo or audio 
systems, operated at unreasonably high-volume levels; or 
 
7. Any other relevant factor or condition that would lead a reasonable person to conclude 
the noise is an unreasonable noise as defined in this section. 
 
E. Any person who violates any provision of this section is guilty of a misdemeanor and shall 
be subject to the following penalties: 
 
1. For a first offense: a fine of not less than one hundred dollars ($100.00) or more than 
two hundred dollars ($200); 
 
2. For a second offense within a twelve (12) month period: a fine of not less than two 
hundred fifty dollars ($250.00) or more than three hundred dollars ($300.00); or 
 
3. For a third or subsequent offense within a twelve (12) month period: a fine of not less 
than four hundred dollars ($400.00) or more than five hundred dollars ($500.00), or by 
imprisonment for not more than six (6) months, or both. Each person cited for a third 
or subsequent offense within a twelve (12) month period shall appear in municipal 
court. 
 
F. This section shall not apply to any person operating loudspeakers, sound cars or sound 
trucks upon any street, alley or public property of the city, provided they have first obtained 
written permission from the police chief and a permit in accordance with Sections 8.56.060 
and 8.56.070 of the city code. 
 
G. For purposes of this section: 
 
1. “Motor vehicle” means as defined by Wyo. Stat. Section 31-5-102(a)(xxiv); 
 
2. “Unreasonable noise” means any sound, by volume or duration, that is or tends to be 
injurious to human health or welfare, or which unreasonably interferes with the quiet 
enjoyment of life or property; and 
 
3. “Vehicle” means as defined by Wyo. Stat. Section 31-5-102(a)(lviii). 
 
Section 2.  That Section 10.08.010, State law adopted, of Chapter 10.08, State Traffic Laws 
Adopted, and Section 10.12.020, Incarceration for certain traffic offenses, of Chapter 10.12, 
Administration and Enforcement, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, are hereby amended to read: 

 
Page 3 of 5 
 
 
10.08.010 - State law adopted. 
 
The following sections of state law as in effect on October 1, 2022, but not including any 
exclusions enumerated are adopted by reference and incorporated as if fully set out in this title. A 
copy of each provision is on file in the office of city clerk. 
 
A. Wyo. Stat. Sections 31-5-101 through and including 31-5-1402 and Section 31-5-1601, 
with the exception of Section Sections 31-5-953 and 31-5-1201, of the Uniform Act 
Regulating Traffic on Highways. 
 
B. Wyo. Stat. Sections 31-7-101 through and including 31-7-138. 
 
C. Wyo. Stat. Sections 31-4-101 through and including 31-4-104, General Offenses and 
Penalties. 
 
D. Wyo. Stat. Sections 31-2-201 through and including 31-2-303, The Title and 
Registration Act. 
 
E. Wyo. Stat. Sections 31-6-102 through and including 31-6-108 of the Implied Consent 
to Chemical Testing Act. 
 
F. Wyo. Stat. Sections 31-8-105, the prohibited acts and penalties of The Identification 
Card Act. 
 
G. Wyo. Stat. Sections 31-7-401 through and including 31-7-404, ignition interlock 
licenses. 
 
 
10.12.020 - Incarceration for certain traffic offenses. 
 
The municipal court may not impose a jail sentence for any traffic-related offense except for 
the following offenses: 
 
A. Reckless driving (city code Section 10.08.010 and Wyo. Stat. Section 31-5-229); 
 
B. Driving or having control of vehicle while under influence of intoxicating liquor or 
controlled substances (city code Section 10.08.010 and Wyo. Stat. Section 31-5-233); 
 
C. Driving while license cancelled, suspended or revoked (city code Section 10.08.010 
and Wyo. Stat. Section 31-7-134); 
 
D. Fleeing or attempting to elude police officers (city code Section 10.08.010 and Wyo. 
Stat. Section 31-5-225); 
 
E. Duty to stop vehicle where accident involves death or personal injuries (city code 
Section 10.08.010 and Wyo. Stat. Section 31-5-1101); and 

 
Page 4 of 5 
 
 
F. Failure to maintain liability coverage (city code Section 10.08.010 and Wyo. Stat. 
Section 31-4-103);. and 
 
G. Vehicle mufflers, excessive smoke and unreasonable noise, for a third or subsequent 
offense within a twelve (12) month period (city code Section 10.24.200). 
 
The municipal court shall advise the defendant that the maximum sentence for any of the 
offenses listed in subsections (A) through (F) of this section is six (6) months in jail, a fine of seven 
hundred fifty dollars ($750.00), or both. For an offense listed in subsection (G) of this section, the 
municipal court shall advise the defendant that the maximum sentence is six (6) months in jail, a 
fine of five hundred dollars ($500.00), or both. 
 
Section 3.  That Section 8.56.050, Motor vehicle mufflers, of Chapter 8.56, Noise Control, 
of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby repealed. 
 
8.56.050 - Motor vehicle mufflers. 
 
No person shall modify, repair or replace the exhaust system of a motor vehicle or operate a 
motor vehicle in a manner which will amplify or increase the noise emitted to a level above which 
will amplify or increase the noise emitted to a level above that emitted by the original exhaust 
system of that vehicle. No muffler or exhaust system shall be equipped with a cutout, bypass, or 
similar device. 
 
Section 4.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 5.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE FILING OF APPLICATIONS 
WITH THE FEDERAL TRANSIT ADMINISTRATION, AN OPERATING 
ADMINISTRATION OF THE UNITED STATES DEPARTMENT OF 
TRANSPORTATION, 
FOR 
FEDERAL 
TRANSPORTATION 
ASSISTANCE AUTHORIZED BY 49 U.S.C. CHAPTER 53; TITLE 23, 
UNITED 
STATES 
CODE, 
OR 
OTHER 
FEDERAL 
STATUTES 
ADMINISTERED BY THE FEDERAL TRANSIT ADMINISTRATION.” 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne, through the Cheyenne Transit Program, operates public 
transportation services that are vital to the mobility, accessibility, and well-being of the City’s 
residents; 
 
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Transit Program receives a significant portion of its operational 
and capital funding through federal transportation assistance programs administered by the FTA, 
including regular funding through the Urbanized Area Formula Program authorized under 49 
U.S.C. § 5307, as well as other FTA programs such as the Bus and Bus Facilities Program (49 
U.S.C. § 5339), the Enhanced Mobility of Seniors and Individuals with Disabilities Program (49 
U.S.C. § 5310), and various discretionary or emergency grant opportunities; 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne has received written authority from the Governor of 
Wyoming, as the designated recipient, to apply for and receive Urbanized Area Formula Program 
assistance; 
 
WHEREAS, the Administrator of the Federal Transit Administration (FTA) has been 
delegated authority to award federal financial assistance for public transportation projects; 
 
WHEREAS, any grant or cooperative agreement for federal financial assistance will 
impose certain obligations upon the applicant, including compliance with federal requirements and 
the provision of matching funds for the project; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne has provided or will provide all annual certifications 
and assurances required by the FTA in connection with such applications. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Mayor, or his designee, is authorized to execute and file 
applications for federal assistance on behalf of the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Transit 
Program with the Federal Transit Administration, for assistance authorized by 49 U.S.C. Chapter 
53, Title 23 of the United States Code, or any other federal statute administered by the FTA; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Mayor, or his designee, is further authorized to 
execute and file with such applications all annual certifications, assurances, and other documents 
required by the Federal Transit Administration before awarding a federal assistance grant or 
cooperative agreement; 
 
9:52 am, Jul 31, 2025

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the City of Cheyenne affirms it has received authority 
from the designated recipient to apply for Urbanized Area Formula Program assistance under 49 
U.S.C. § 5307; and 
 
BE IT FINALLY RESOLVED, that the Mayor, or his designee, is authorized to execute 
grant agreements, cooperative agreements, and related documents with the Federal Transit 
Administration on behalf of the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Transit Program, and to take 
any other actions necessary to carry out the purpose of this resolution. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS _______ DAY OF __________________, 
2025. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST:  
 
____________________________________  
Kylie Soden, City Clerk

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2025-08-05_TotalExtraction_87565727.pdf
 
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE SUBMISSION OF A FEDERAL 
MINERAL ROYALTY CAPITAL CONSTRUCTION ACCOUNT GRANT 
APPLICATION TO THE STATE LOAN AND INVESTMENT BOARD ON 
BEHALF OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE FOR THE PURCHASE OF A 
TYPE 6 WILDLAND BRUSH TRUCK WITH EQUIPMENT.” 
 
WHEREAS, the State of Wyoming’s Mineral Royalty Grant (MRG) program 
provides that federal mineral royalties may be loaned or granted to incorporated cities and 
towns, counties, and special districts for various purposes, including to provide an essential 
public service; 
 
 WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne desires to apply for an 
MRG Federal Mineral Royalty Capital Construction Account Grant to assist in financing 
the purchase of a Type 6 Wildland Brush Truck with equipment, at a total estimated cost 
of three hundred seventy-five thousand dollars ($375,000.00); 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body recognizes the critical need for this acquisition 
in order to maintain essential public safety services; 
 
WHEREAS, applications for the Federal Mineral Royalty Capital Construction 
Account Grant must meet the eligibility criteria set forth in the State Loan and Investment 
Board’s Rules and Regulations, and to the best of the Governing Body’s knowledge, this 
application satisfies those requirements; and 
 
WHEREAS, the program requires a fifty percent (50%) local funding match, and 
the City plans to provide one hundred eighty-seven thousand five hundred dollars 
($187,500) from the two thousand twenty-one (2021) Special Purpose Option Tax/Grants 
Match Fund for this purpose. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF 
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that a grant application in the amount of one 
hundred eighty-seven thousand five hundred dollars ($187,500) be submitted to the State 
Loan and Investment Board for consideration at its February 3, 2026, meeting, to fund fifty 
percent (50%) of the total project cost of three hundred seventy-five thousand dollars 
($375,000.00) for the purchase of a Type 6 Wildland Brush Truck with equipment; and 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Mayor is hereby authorized to execute any 
grant award documents, agreements, or related instruments, including any and all 
subsequent grant award documents, on behalf of the City, without further action by the 
Governing Body, provided such documents are consistent with this Resolution and the 
terms of the approved grant. 
 
9:47 am, Aug 15, 2025

20__.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
August 6, 2025 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #S-29-25 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and 
BCM, Inc. d/b/a Mortensen Electric, for the Dutcher Baseball Fields Lighting 
Installation (East Fields) project 
______________________________________________ 
Bid #S-29-25 was solicited for the Dutcher Baseball Fields Lighting Installation (East Fields) project. 
 
Four (4) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.  The Bids were received 
from: 
1. 
BCM Inc dba Mortenson Electric 
2. 
Faith Enterprises Incorporated 
3. 
High Plains Energy, Inc. dba Pinnacle Electric & Construction 
4. 
Wescom, Inc. 
 
The bids were reviewed by Jason Sanchez (CRE), Sam Payne (CRE), and Brandon Peterson (CRE), 
and their recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid from BCM, Inc. d/b/a Mortensen 
Electric. 
 
The total Bid Award is $662,000.00. 
 
Project Completion is scheduled for March 2026. 
 
Funding:  
One Time State Distribution Funds 
001-21-2113-40-43701 (Park and Rec Projects) 
$662,000.00 
 

 
 

City of Cheyenne 
Purchasing 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
2101 O'Neil Ave., Room 309, Cheyenne, WY 82001

IFB No. S-29-25

RESPONSE DEADLINE: July 29, 2025 at 2:00 pm 
Report Generated:  Tuesday, July 29, 2025

CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
AGREEMENT 
 
Bid No. S-29-25 
 
Contract No. _______ 
 
THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, 2025, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and BCM INC., 
D/B/A MORTENSEN ELECTRIC, hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the “Dutcher Baseball Fields Lighting Installation (East Fields)” 
project all in strict accordance with the Contract Documents, including all addenda thereto, 
numbered and dated: Addendum One (1), dated July 15, 2025; and Addendum Two (2), dated 
July 15, 2025. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract, or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City. The work shall be completed by March 1, 2026. If the work has not been completed 
within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City for excusable 
delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated damages, as 
stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the work is 
completed. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, in the amount of six 
hundred sixty-two thousand dollars ($662,000.00).  In the event there are changes in the 
estimated quantities shown on the Bid Proposal, the unit prices multiplied by the actual quantities 
shall govern, and the total contract amount will be adjusted accordingly. The City agrees to pay 
the above amount for contractual services in the following manner, upon receipt of appropriate 
documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for the percentage of 
estimated work completed. Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the 
payable due date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office. The pay request 
shall be submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or 
12:42 pm, Aug 01, 2025

Page 2 of 2 
the AIA Documents G702 and G703. The engineer will review the estimate for approval 
prior to payment. 
 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116. Upon completion of the work 
under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of Completion; 
the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all Sub-Contractors, 
Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of Payment; and a 
current Workers Compensation Certificate. Final payment will not be made until the above 
documents have been received by the City, all items on the Punch List have been 
completed, and the advertising requirements have been met. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  CONTRACT.  The executed Contract Documents shall consist of the 
following: 
 
1. This Agreement;  
2. Addenda; 
3. Invitation for Bids; 
4. Instructions to Bidders; 
5. Signed Bid Proposal; 
 
6. General Conditions and Insurance; 
7. Supplemental Conditions; 
8. Part IV Forms & Notices; 
9. Specifications and Special 
Provisions; and  
10. Drawings. 
 
This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said 
other documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms 
the Contract between the parties hereto. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, that the governing body of the City of Cheyenne has authorized 
the Mayor as Executive Officer of the City to enter into this Agreement, and that the parties hereto 
have caused this Agreement to be executed on the day and year in the first part herein written. 
 
ATTEST: 
 
 
 
 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING

Patrick Collins, Mayor

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2025-06-02_TotalExtraction_34354220.pdf
1 
 
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
June 9, 2025  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting. Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich (via Zoom), Dr. Kathy Emmons, 
Ken Esquibel (via Zoom), Pete Laybourn, Mark Moody, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom 
Segrave and Jeff White. Also present: Kylie Soden, City Clerk. The pledge of allegiance was 
recited. 
 
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received 
by Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Moody, item #24(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Segrave 
moved to approve the remaining items, seconded by Mr. Roybal. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on May 27, 2025. Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for about 199.67 acres of land generally 
located east of South Greeley Highway and north of Chalk Bluff Road. Public comment was made 
by: Mark Christensen, AVI PC, agent for the applicant. Comments were made by the following 
members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Roybal. There being no further comments 
or input and the purpose of the public hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public 
hearing. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for proposed budgets for the City of Cheyenne and 
Board of Public Utilities for Fiscal Year 2026. There being no comments or input and the purpose 
of the public hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, for the various purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing 
the amount of general and special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said 
appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2025, and ending June 30, 2026. Reporting 
for the Committee of the Whole, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded 
by Mr. White. Dr. Rinne moved to amend by substitute dated June 5, 2025, seconded by Mr. 
Roybal. Motion to amend by substitute carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
Main motion as amended carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the 
exception of Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Moody voting “no”. Public comment was made 

2 
 
by: Erin LeBlanc and Katie Brady, Laramie County Senior Services. Comments were made by the 
following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Mr. Laybourn and Mr. 
Moody. The following is a summary of the proposed budget, including the aforementioned 
amendments: General Fund, $74,517,876; Weed and Pest, $801,700; Youth Alternatives Grants, 
$919,234; Recreation Programs, $2,656,270, Community Development Block Grant, $660,183; 
Law Enforcement Grants, $267,441; Miscellaneous Federal Grants, $1,125,070; Transportation 
Planning, $1,805,225; Transit, $6,023,629; Juvenile Justice, $280,726; Youth Alternatives 
Mentoring, $73,794; Development Impact Fees, $1,531,100; One Percent Sales Tax, $13,878,876; 
Specific Purpose Option Tax, $595,554; Youth Activities, $19,195; Golf Facilities, $570,700; 
Fleet Maintenance, $7,090,211; Solid Waste Management, $21,513,486; Civic Center, 
$2,369,999; Ice & Events Center, $970,215; Surface Water Mitigation, $5,983,757; Perpetual 
Care, $16,400; Self-Insurance $11,203,838; Total City, $154,874,481; Water Enterprise, 
$31,851,815; Sewer Enterprise, $16,920,270; Total Appropriation, $203,646,566. (#4624) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Approving and establishing revised water and sewer 
rates, tap and system development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties, and 
septic and commercial waste dump rates, as recommended by the Board of Public Utilities. 
Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Laybourn moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with 
the exception of Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Moody voting “no”. Comments were made by the following 
members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich and Dr. Rinne. (#4625) 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending portions of the Unified Development Code, 
modifying provisions related to Park Impact Fees, including the timing of fee payment and 
permitted uses of such fees, and making conforming amendments thereto. Reporting for Public 
Services Committee, Mr. Segrave moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White. 
Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending portions of the Unified Development Code 
regarding signage, to increase allowances for window signs and further clarifying prohibited signs. 
Mr. Segrave moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White. Dr. Rinne moved to 
amend Section 3, Pedestrian Signs, bullet point three to read “Window Signs shall be allowed in 
any window. Window signs shall be counted toward the allowed wall sign area. If 100% of the 
window area is to be covered, it must utilize a material that allows light to penetrate into the 
building”, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Motion to amend carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich voting “no”. Main motion as amended carried. 
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. Comments were made by the following 
members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins and Dr. Rinne. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, various 
tracts of land completely surrounded by current city limits situate in northeast Cheyenne generally 
located north of Dell Range Boulevard, east and west of Ridge Road and south of Storey 
Boulevard. Mr. Segrave moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Dr. Emmons. Motion 
carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Aldrich and 
Mr. Moody voting “no”. Public comment was made by: Daniel Singh. Comments were made by 

3 
 
the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Dr. Emmons, Mr. 
Esquibel, Mr. Moody, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Segrave. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classifications of: AG – Agriculture; MR – Medium-Density 
Residential; MUR – Mixed-Use Residential; and, CB – Community Business, for land annexed to 
the City of Cheyenne generally located north of Dell Range Boulevard, east and west of Ridge 
Road and south of Storey Boulevard. Mr. Segrave moved to approve on second reading, seconded 
by Mr. White. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the 
exception of Mr. Moody voting “no”. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion 
of land generally located east of South Greeley Highway and north of Chalk Bluff Road – referred 
to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Segrave) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne to assign a Zoning Classification of AG Agricultural to a parcel of land annexed to the 
City, generally located east of South Greeley Highway and north of Chalk Bluff Road – referred 
to Public Services Committee. (Sponsors – Mr. Moody and Mr. Segrave) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Section 10.04.050, Obedience Required, of 
the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to clarify the default offense level for 
violations of Title 10 of the Municipal Code – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – 
Mr. Segrave) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Section 6.08.180, Feeding Geese and Ducks 
Prohibited, of Chapter 6.08, Animal Care and Control Regulations Generally, of Title 6, Animals, 
of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to include seagulls and extend the 
feeding prohibition to all property within city limits – referred to Public Services Committee.

Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne 
Downtown Development Authority for professional and administrative services for the fiscal year 
beginning July 1, 2025 and ending June 30, 2026 (General Fund). Dr. Rinne moved to approve, 
seconded by Mr. White. Dr. Rinne moved to amend section 4(a)(i) to remove “the Director of 
Planning and Development Department” in the first sentence and replace with “the DDA Board”,

body. Dr. Rinne moved to amend by substitute dated June 9, 2025, seconded by Mr. Roybal. 
Motion to amend by substitute carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. Main 
motion as twice amended carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the

Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held June 23, 2025 at 6:00 p.m. in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O'Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference meeting, 
for the 2025-2027 City of Cheyenne Comprehensive Consolidated Plan and the 2025 Annual 
Action Plan, as prepared by Cheyenne Housing and Community Development. 
 
The following agreement was introduced under Other Business and referred to the June 17,
